## GHSA-x585-4vv8-2mh2 · rucio

2026-11-13 · Python · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The system incorrectly allowed SSH challenge tokens, which are intended as nonces for signing, to be used directly as authentication tokens. This design flaw meant that a token meant for a cryptographic challenge could be re-purposed to authenticate a request without proper signature verification.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could impersonate an administrator by presenting a valid SSH challenge token, gaining full administrative control over the Rucio instance.

### Key diff

```
# SSH challenge tokens share this table but are nonces to be signed, not
    # credentials, so they must never authenticate a request on their own.
    if token.startswith('challenge-'):
        raise CannotAuthenticate("Challenge tokens cannot be used for authentication.")
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check at the beginning of the token validation process. If a token starts with 'challenge-', it is immediately rejected with a 'CannotAuthenticate' error, preventing challenge tokens from being used as direct authentication credentials.

## GHSA-6vhm-j9h7-f9gh · AutoMapper

2026-11-13 · C# · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from AutoMapper's `TypeMapPlanBuilder` not correctly incrementing the depth counter for mapping operations when constructor parameters were involved. The `IncTypeDepthInfo` call was placed after `createDestination`, meaning that child objects mapped within constructor parameters were not accounted for in the depth check, leading to infinite recursion if a circular dependency existed.

### Impact

An attacker could craft an object graph with circular dependencies that, when mapped by AutoMapper, would lead to an uncontrolled recursion, causing a stack overflow and resulting in a denial of service for the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/AutoMapper/Execution/TypeMapPlanBuilder.cs
+++ b/src/AutoMapper/Execution/TypeMapPlanBuilder.cs
@@ -207,15 +207,17 @@ private Expression CreateDestinationFunc()
 
     private Expression CreateAssignmentFunc(Expression createDestination)
     {
-        List<Expression> actions = [createDestination];
+        List<Expression> actions = [];
         Expression typeMapExpression = null;
         var hasMaxDepth = _typeMap.MaxDepth > 0;
         if (hasMaxDepth)
         {
+            // Count this level before creating the destination, constructor parameters map child objects there
             typeMapExpression = Constant(_typeMap);
             actions.Add(Call(ContextParameter, IncTypeDepthInfo, typeMapExpression));
         }
 
+        actions.Add(createDestination);
         AddBeforeMap(actions);
         AddPropertyMaps(actions);
         AddPathMaps(actions);
```

### Fix summary

The patch moves the `IncTypeDepthInfo` call, which increments the depth counter, to occur before the `createDestination` expression. This ensures that the depth is correctly incremented for the current mapping level, including any child objects mapped within constructor parameters, thereby preventing infinite recursion.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 38 occurrences**

## GHSA-gpwp-5797-mjff · AutoMapper

2026-11-13 · C# · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→INFINITE_LOOP`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from uncontrolled recursion during object mapping, specifically when handling derived types or member maps. The `MaxDepth` configuration, intended to limit recursion, was not correctly propagated to derived type maps, allowing an attacker to craft an object graph that would bypass this limit and cause a stack overflow.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted object graph to AutoMapper, leading to an `InsufficientExecutionStackException` and causing the application to crash, resulting in a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/AutoMapper/Mapper.cs
+++ b/src/AutoMapper/Mapper.cs
@@ -192,6 +192,8 @@ private TDestination MapWithOptions<TSource, TDestination>(TSource source, TDest
     private TDestination MapCore<TSource, TDestination>(
         TSource source, TDestination destination, ResolutionContext context, Type sourceType = null, Type destinationType = null, MemberMap memberMap = null)
     {
+        // Runtime map calls can recurse as deep as the source graph, so fail before the stack overflows
+        System.Runtime.CompilerServices.RuntimeHelpers.EnsureSufficientExecutionStack();
         TypePair requestedTypes = new(typeof(TSource), typeof(TDestination));
```

### Fix summary

The patch ensures that `MaxDepth` is correctly inherited by derived type maps. It also introduces `System.Runtime.CompilerServices.RuntimeHelpers.EnsureSufficientExecutionStack()` at the beginning of mapping operations to proactively check for stack exhaustion. Additionally, it modifies exception handling to avoid re-wrapping `InsufficientExecutionStackException` to prevent further stack usage when the stack is already nearly exhausted.

## GHSA-mmhr-3c5m-f64f · soupsieve

2026-11-13 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an inefficient algorithm used for the descendant combinator in CSS matching. When processing deeply nested HTML structures with multiple descendant combinators, the algorithm would repeatedly traverse the ancestor chain for each combinator, leading to a polynomial time complexity proportional to the depth of the document and the number of combinators. This lack of caching for ancestor checks caused redundant computations.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a specially designed HTML document and CSS selector that, when processed by Soup Sieve, would consume excessive CPU resources and time, leading to a denial of service for applications using the library.

### Key diff

```
--- a/soupsieve/css_match.py
+++ b/soupsieve/css_match.py
@@ -998,9 +1025,12 @@ def match_past_relations(self, el: bs4.Tag, relation: ct.SelectorList) -> MatchG
             return found
 
         if relation[0].rel_type == REL_PARENT:
-            parent: bs4.Tag | None = el
-            while not found and parent and (parent := self.get_parent(parent)):
-                found = parent is not None and (yield parent, relation)
+            if self.enable_cache:
+                found = yield from self.match_ancestor(el, relation)
+            else:
+                parent: bs4.Tag | None = el
+                while not found and parent and (parent := self.get_parent(parent)):
+                    found = parent is not None and (yield parent, relation)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an ancestor cache (`anc_cache`) to store the results of ancestor checks. A new `match_ancestor` method utilizes this cache to avoid redundant computations when traversing the parent chain for descendant combinators. This significantly optimizes the performance for deeply nested structures, reducing the complexity from polynomial to linear.

## GHSA-qfx6-q44v-9qqw · ag-psd

2026-11-13 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `ag-psd` library did not properly validate the dimensions (width, height) of PSD files, particularly for layer and mask data. An attacker could craft a PSD file with excessively large dimensions, leading to the allocation of huge amounts of memory when the library attempted to create `ImageData` objects or canvases, ultimately exhausting available memory.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted PSD file to an application using `ag-psd`, causing the application to consume all available memory and crash, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/psdReader.ts
+++ b/src/psdReader.ts
@@ -234,6 +234,7 @@ export function readPsd(reader: PsdReader, readOptions: ReadOptions = {}) {
 	Object.assign(reader, readOptions);
 	reader.large = version === 2;
 	reader.globalAlpha = false;
+	if (!reader.totalMemoryLimit) reader.totalMemoryLimit = 2 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024; // default 2GB memory limit
 
 	// color mode data
 	readSection(reader, 1, left => {
@@ -580,14 +581,15 @@ export function decodeLayerImageData(layer: Layer, useImageData?: boolean, throw
 	const cmyk = colorMode === ColorMode.CMYK;
 
 	let imageData: PixelData | undefined;
+	let maskData: PixelData | undefined;
 	let initializedAlpha = false;
 
-	if (layerWidth && layerHeight) {
+	if (layerWidth && layerHeight && read === LayerDataType.Layer) {
 		if (cmyk) {
 			if (bitsPerChannel !== 8) throw new Error('bitsPerChannel Not supproted');
 			imageData = { width: layerWidth, height: layerHeight, data: new Uint8ClampedArray(layerWidth * layerHeight * 5) } as any as ImageData;
 		} else {
-			imageData = createImageDataBitDepth(layerWidth, layerHeight, bitsPerChannel);
+			imageData = createImageDataBitDepth(layerWidth, layerHeight, bitsPerChannel, 4, memoryLimit);
 		}
 	}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `totalMemoryLimit` to the `PsdReader` with a default of 2GB. It also modifies the `createImageDataBitDepth` function to accept and enforce this memory limit, preventing the allocation of excessively large image buffers. Additionally, the logic for decoding layer and mask data was refactored to better manage memory and apply the new limits.

## GHSA-vvf4-9x63-8xvv · dnspython

2026-11-13 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The dnspython library's WKS record parsing did not properly validate the size of the service bitmap. An attacker could provide a WKS record with an extremely large bitmap, leading to excessive memory allocation and CPU usage during processing.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious DNS WKS record that, when processed by a system using dnspython, would consume significant memory and CPU resources, potentially leading to a denial of service for the application or system.

### Key diff

```
--- a/dns/rdtypes/IN/WKS.py
+++ b/dns/rdtypes/IN/WKS.py
@@ -45,6 +45,10 @@ def __init__(self, rdclass, rdtype, address, protocol, bitmap):
         self.address = self._as_ipv4_address(address)
         self.protocol = self._as_uint8(protocol)
         self.bitmap = self._as_bytes(bitmap)
+        if len(self.bitmap) > 8192:
+            raise ValueError(
+                "service bitmap must be between 0 and 8192 bytes inclusive"
+            )
 
     def to_styled_text(self, style: dns.rdata.RdataStyle) -> str:
         bits = []
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a size limit of 8192 bytes for the service bitmap in WKS records. It also validates that service numbers are within a valid range (0-65535) and optimizes the bitmap extension logic to prevent excessive memory allocation when processing large service numbers.

## GHSA-r99w-jh2x-3mg9 · @nuxt/nitro-server

2026-11-13 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.5 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The previous fix for GHSA-9v7c-q64q-vv33 was incomplete. It failed to properly normalize request paths that started with `//`, leading to an incorrect interpretation of the URL. This allowed an attacker to bypass `server/middleware` path checks and server route rules.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass security middleware and route rules, potentially gaining unauthorized access to pages or endpoints that should have been protected. This could lead to information disclosure or other unauthorized actions.

### Key diff

```
-function toWebView (event: H3Event, resolveURL = () => new URL(encodeEventPath(event.path), `${getRequestProtocol(event, { xForwardedProto: false })}://${getRequestHost(event)}`)): RendererEvent {
+function toWebView (event: H3Event, resolveURL = () => resolveEventURL(event)): RendererEvent {
   const node = event.node
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `resolveEventURL` function that explicitly constructs the URL by first building a base URL with protocol and host, and then assigning the `pathname` from the event's path after encoding. This ensures that paths starting with `//` are correctly interpreted as part of the path, rather than being treated as a host, thus enforcing proper path checks.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 106 occurrences**

## GHSA-jf9x-2fg2-hx4w · @sveltejs/adapter-static

2026-11-13 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The SvelteKit adapters for Vercel and static deployments generated route configurations where prerendered paths were directly used in regular expressions without proper escaping. This allowed special characters in user-controlled paths to be interpreted as regex metacharacters.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious path that, when prerendered, would generate a route configuration with an overly broad regular expression. This could lead to unintended routing, potentially allowing access to restricted pages or causing denial of service by matching a large number of legitimate routes.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/adapter-cloudflare/utils.js
+++ b/packages/adapter-cloudflare/utils.js
@@ -135,7 +181,10 @@ export function get_routes_json(builder, client_assets, redirects, routes) {
 		}
 
 		if (rule === '<prerendered>') {
-			builder.prerendered.paths.forEach((path) => excluded_routes.add(path));
+			// paths with wildcards are left to the Worker, which serves prerendered paths by exact match
+			for (const path of builder.prerendered.paths) {
+				if (!has_wildcard(path)) excluded_routes.add(path);
+			}
 			continue;
 		}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `has_wildcard` function to detect paths containing regex-significant characters like `*` or `:[A-Za-z]`. Prerendered redirects with such wildcards are now skipped from being added to the `_redirects` file, and paths with wildcards are excluded from the `_routes.json` file, preventing them from being interpreted as regex patterns.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 32 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, JavaScript, Python, PHP, Java, Ruby

> → GHSA-cjw9-ghj4-fwxf

## GHSA-mjrq-cffv-q4qc · @nuxt/schema

2026-11-13 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→DIRECTORY_LISTING`

### Root cause

The Nuxt dev server incorrectly determined the project's workspace root by relying on the `pkg-types` library's `findWorkspaceDir` function. This function, when used with default or broad options, could traverse up the directory tree beyond the intended project root and identify a parent directory as the workspace if it contained certain lockfiles or workspace configuration files. Consequently, the dev server would serve files from this broader, unintended directory.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially access sensitive files or configuration from parent directories outside the intended project scope, leading to information disclosure. This could expose internal project structures, credentials, or other confidential data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/schema/src/config/common.ts
+++ b/packages/schema/src/config/common.ts
@@ -5,11 +5,11 @@ import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto'
 import { relative, resolve } from 'pathe'
 import { isDebug, isDevelopment, isTest } from 'std-env'
 import { defu } from 'defu'
-import { findWorkspaceDir } from 'pkg-types'
 
 import type { NuxtDebugOptions } from '../types/debug.ts'
 import type { NuxtModule } from '../types/module.ts'
 import { defineResolvers } from '../utils/definition.ts'
+import { findWorkspaceDir } from '../utils/workspace-dir.ts'
 import { DEFAULT_JS_FILE_EXTENSIONS } from '../constants.ts'
 
 export default defineResolvers({
@@ -24,10 +24,7 @@ export default defineResolvers({
       const rootDir = await get('rootDir')
       return val && typeof val === 'string'
         ? resolve(rootDir, val)
-        : findWorkspaceDir(rootDir, {
-            gitConfig: 'closest',
-            try: true,
-          }).catch(() => rootDir)
+        : findWorkspaceDir(rootDir)
     },
   },
   srcDir: {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a custom `findWorkspaceDir` utility within `@nuxt/schema` that implements a more restrictive logic for identifying the workspace root. Instead of relying on `pkg-types`, it now explicitly checks for specific workspace declaration files (e.g., `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `lerna.json`, `turbo.json`, `deno.json`) or a `.git/config` file, ensuring the workspace boundary is correctly identified and preventing traversal into unintended parent directories.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 8 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, Python, Go, PHP, C#

> → GHSA-g39v-cvjh-8fpf
> → GHSA-3jmg-p96m-m328
> → GHSA-rq7w-g337-39qq
> → GHSA-wf69-r4mx-43rr
> → GHSA-rxrh-4j9h-xgg9
> → GHSA-4wwp-f6gw-6qm5

## GHSA-rqw3-x7hx-mm3p · @sveltejs/adapter-node

2026-11-13 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.9 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability arose because the adapter attempted to modify headers on a `Response` object that was immutable, specifically when the `Response` was returned from a `fetch` call. This immutable header modification attempt led to a crash in the Node.js process.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a server crash by sending a request that results in an immutable `Response` object with a `content-type` of `text/event-stream`, leading to a denial of service for the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/adapter-node/src/handler.js
+++ b/packages/adapter-node/src/handler.js
 	// that don't recognise it. See https://github.com/sveltejs/kit/issues/15790
 	// The header is set on the Node response rather than the `Response` object,
 	// whose headers may be immutable (e.g. when returned from `fetch`)
 	if (response.headers.get('content-type') === 'text/event-stream') {
-		response.headers.set('x-accel-buffering', 'no');
+		res.setHeader('x-accel-buffering', 'no');
 	}
 
 	setResponse(res, response);
```

### Fix summary

The patch changes where the `x-accel-buffering` header is set. Instead of attempting to set it on the potentially immutable `Response` object, it now sets the header directly on the Node.js `res` (response) object, which is mutable and avoids the crash.

## GHSA-3v2p-hm54-8qcq · @opentelemetry/instrumentation-http

2026-11-13 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.8 · Pattern: `CREDENTIAL_LEAK→LOG_EXPOSURE`

### Root cause

The OpenTelemetry HTTP instrumentation library was not redacting certain sensitive query parameters from URLs in logs and traces by default. Specifically, AWS-related authentication parameters like 'X-Amz-Signature', 'X-Amz-Credential', and 'X-Amz-Security-Token' were missing from the default redaction list.

### Impact

An attacker with access to logs or traces could potentially extract sensitive authentication information, leading to unauthorized access to AWS resources or other services if these credentials are reused.

### Key diff

```
--- a/experimental/packages/opentelemetry-instrumentation-http/src/internal-types.ts
+++ b/experimental/packages/opentelemetry-instrumentation-http/src/internal-types.ts
@@ -60,4 +60,7 @@ export const DEFAULT_QUERY_STRINGS_TO_REDACT = [
   'Signature',
   'AWSAccessKeyId',
   'X-Goog-Signature',
+  'X-Amz-Signature',
+  'X-Amz-Credential',
+  'X-Amz-Security-Token',
 ] as const;
```

### Fix summary

The patch extends the default list of query parameters to be redacted to include AWS-specific authentication parameters. It also refactors the redaction logic into a dedicated `redactQueryString` function and introduces separate configuration options for redacting query parameters on client (outgoing) and server (incoming) spans.

## GHSA-7g44-g9rc-x53w · soupsieve

2026-11-13 · Python · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `find_bidi` function, which recursively traversed the DOM to determine text directionality, did not properly handle deeply nested HTML structures. When processing a crafted HTML document with excessive nesting, the recursive calls would exhaust the Python call stack, leading to a stack overflow.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted HTML document to a system using the `soupsieve` library, causing the application to crash due to a stack overflow, effectively leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/soupsieve/css_match.py
+++ b/soupsieve/css_match.py
-        for node in self.get_children(el):
-            if self.is_tag(node):
-                value = self.find_bidi(node)
+        stack = [self.get_children(el)]
+        while stack:
+            for node in stack[-1]:
```

### Fix summary

The fix refactors the `find_bidi` function from a recursive approach to an iterative one using an explicit stack. This change prevents the Python call stack from being exhausted by deeply nested HTML structures, mitigating the denial of service vulnerability.

## GHSA-cpgf-5mjf-73p7 · rails-html-sanitizer

2026-11-13 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an inefficient regular expression used in the `rails-html-sanitizer` gem, specifically within the `scrub_attribute` method. When processing certain crafted HTML inputs, this regex could lead to excessive backtracking, consuming significant CPU resources.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious HTML input that, when processed by the sanitizer, would cause the server to spend an inordinate amount of time evaluating the regular expression, leading to a denial of service for the application.

### Key diff

```
-
          return if Loofah::HTML5::SafeList::ATTR_VAL_IS_URI.include?(attr_name) && Loofah::HTML5::Scrub.scrub_uri_attribute(attr_node)
-
-          if Loofah::HTML5::SafeList::SVG_ATTR_VAL_ALLOWS_REF.include?(attr_name)
-            Loofah::HTML5::Scrub.scrub_attribute_that_allows_local_ref(attr_node)
-          end
-
-          if Loofah::HTML5::SafeList::SVG_ALLOW_LOCAL_HREF.include?(node.name) && Loofah::HTML5::SafeList::SVG_HREF_ATTRIBUTES.include?(attr_name) && attr_node.value =~ /^\s*[^#\s].*/m
-            attr_node.remove
-          end
+
+          return if Loofah::HTML5::Scrub.scrub_attribute_value(node, attr_node, attr_name)
```

### Fix summary

The patch updates the `loofah` dependency to version `2.25.3` or higher. This new version of `loofah` contains an optimized `scrub_attribute_value` method and refactors the attribute scrubbing logic to avoid the problematic regular expression, thereby mitigating the ReDoS vulnerability.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 32 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, JavaScript, Python, PHP, Java, Ruby

## GHSA-mf5v-w3w3-f6cg · opentelemetry-api

2026-11-13 · Python · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the OpenTelemetry Python API's traceparent parsing, which could exhibit quadratic time complexity. This meant that specially crafted, long traceparent headers could consume excessive CPU resources, leading to a denial of service.

### Impact

An attacker could send malicious traceparent headers to an application using the OpenTelemetry Python API, causing the application to become unresponsive or crash due to high CPU utilization, thereby denying service to legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/opentelemetry-sdk/src/opentelemetry/sdk/metrics/_internal/export/__init__.py
+++ b/opentelemetry-sdk/src/opentelemetry/sdk/metrics/_internal/export/__init__.py
@@ -287,6 +330,20 @@ def __init__(
                 else:
                     raise Exception(f"Invalid instrument class found {typ}")
 
+        self._instrument_class_cardinality_limit = {
+            _Counter: _DEFAULT_CARDINALITY_LIMIT,
+            _UpDownCounter: _DEFAULT_CARDINALITY_LIMIT,
+            _Histogram: _DEFAULT_CARDINALITY_LIMIT,
+            _Gauge: _DEFAULT_CARDINALITY_LIMIT,
+            _ObservableCounter: _DEFAULT_CARDINALITY_LIMIT,
+            _ObservableUpDownCounter: _DEFAULT_CARDINALITY_LIMIT,
+            _ObservableGauge: _DEFAULT_CARDINALITY_LIMIT,
+        }
+
+        if cardinality_limits is not None:
+            for typ, limit in cardinality_limits.items():
+                self._instrument_class_cardinality_limit[_validate_cardinality_limit(typ, limit)] = limit
+
         self._otel_component_type = otel_component_type.value if otel_component_type else type(self).__qualname__
         self._metrics = create_metric_reader_metrics(
             self._otel_component_type,
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces cardinality limits for metric instruments, preventing an excessive number of distinct attribute sets from being aggregated. It adds a `cardinality_limits` parameter to `MetricReader` and its subclasses, allowing users to configure maximum limits for different instrument types, thus mitigating resource exhaustion.

## GHSA-q7gw-24fr-5p69 · go.opentelemetry.io/otel/trace

2026-11-13 · Go · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an incorrect handling of valid U+FFFD runes during string truncation. The `truncate` function, intended to limit attribute string length, would incorrectly skip counting valid U+FFFD runes if they appeared after an invalid byte, leading to an underestimation of the string's effective length. This allowed an attacker to craft attribute values that bypassed the length limit.

### Impact

An attacker could provide attribute values that exceed the intended length limits, potentially leading to increased memory consumption or other resource exhaustion issues in systems processing these OpenTelemetry attributes, resulting in a Denial of Service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/internal/shared/attrnorm/truncate.go.tmpl
+++ b/internal/shared/attrnorm/truncate.go.tmpl
@@ -363,14 +363,23 @@ func truncate(limit int, s string) string {
 		if limit == 0 {
 			return ""
 		}
+		// Range reports RuneError for both an invalid byte and a valid U+FFFD.
+		// Only a size-1 decoding indicates invalid UTF-8.
 		_, size := utf8.DecodeRuneInString(s[i:])
 		if size == 1 {
-			// Invalid encoding.
+			// Removing this byte makes len(s)-1 an upper bound for a rebuilt
+			// result. Keep the valid prefix before rescanning s[i:].
 			b.Grow(len(s) - 1)
 			_, _ = b.WriteString(s[:i])
 			s = s[i:]
 			break
 		}
+
+		// A valid U+FFFD still consumes one character of the limit.
+		count++
+		if count > limit {
+			return s[:i]
+		}
 	}
 
 	// Fast-path, no invalid input.
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `truncate` function to correctly count valid U+FFFD runes towards the length limit, even when they appear after an invalid byte. It ensures that the `count` variable is incremented for all valid runes, including U+FFFD, and that truncation occurs once the limit is reached, regardless of the rune's value.

## GHSA-4fqr-96q8-xrfx · sqlite3

2026-11-13 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 4.8 · Pattern: `INTEGER_OVERFLOW→BOUNDARY`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an integer overflow. The `RSTRING_LEN` macro, which returns the length of a Ruby string, could return a value larger than what an `int` type could hold (4 GiB or more). When this length was cast to an `int` for SQLite C API calls, it would truncate the actual length, leading SQLite to process only a portion of the provided string.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially craft SQL queries or bind parameters that, when truncated, alter the intended logic of the query. This could lead to unexpected query behavior, data manipulation, or bypass of security controls, depending on how the truncated string is interpreted by SQLite.

### Key diff

```
-                 (int)RSTRING_LEN(sql),
+                 RSTRING_LENINT(sql),
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the issue by replacing the explicit cast to `(int)RSTRING_LEN(value)` with `RSTRING_LENINT(value)`. The `RSTRING_LENINT` macro is designed to safely convert the string length to an `int` type, preventing truncation for strings larger than 2 GiB (on 64-bit systems) or 4 GiB (on 32-bit systems) and ensuring the correct length is passed to SQLite API functions.

## GHSA-h7wh-q2qr-458c · rails-html-sanitizer

2026-11-13 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 4.7 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from a shared state in the `PermitScrubber` object. When multiple concurrent requests attempted to sanitize HTML, they would modify the same `tags` and `attributes` properties of a single `PermitScrubber` instance, leading to a race condition where one request's intended sanitization rules could be overridden by another's.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass HTML sanitization rules, potentially injecting malicious HTML (e.g., script tags) into the output, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) attacks on users viewing the unsanitized content.

### Key diff

```
-	@permit_scrubber = PermitScrubber.new(prune: prune)
+	@prune = prune
...
-	@permit_scrubber.tags = allowed_tags(options)
-	@permit_scrubber.attributes = allowed_attributes(options)
-	fragment.scrub!(@permit_scrubber)
+	permit_scrubber = PermitScrubber.new(prune: @prune)
+	permit_scrubber.tags = allowed_tags(options)
+	permit_scrubber.attributes = allowed_attributes(options)
+	fragment.scrub!(permit_scrubber)
```

### Fix summary

The patch resolves the issue by ensuring that a new `PermitScrubber` instance is created for each call to the `scrub` method. This eliminates the shared state, preventing concurrent requests from interfering with each other's sanitization rules and ensuring that each sanitization operation uses its own isolated scrubber configuration.

## GHSA-9264-jvgf-6w2f · russh

2026-11-13 · RUST · **LOW** 3.7 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The `kex_done` callback, which exposes the shared secret, was invoked before the server host-key verification was completed. This meant that a malicious server could provide a shared secret to the client's application handler even if its host key was not verified or was rejected.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially trick a client into accepting a shared secret from an unverified or malicious server, leading to an application-level compromise if the application uses this shared secret for further security-sensitive operations.

### Key diff

```
--- a/russh/src/client/mod.rs
+++ b/russh/src/client/mod.rs
-                    let shared_secret = newkeys.kex.shared_secret_bytes();
-                    handler
-                        .kex_done(shared_secret, &newkeys.names, session)
-                        .await?;
+
                     if session.common.encrypted.is_some() {
                         // This is a rekey
+                        handler
+                            .kex_done(newkeys.kex.shared_secret_bytes(), &newkeys.names, session)
+                            .await?;
```

### Fix summary

The patch reorders the execution flow so that the `kex_done` callback is now called only after the server host-key verification has successfully completed. This ensures that the shared secret is not exposed to the application handler if the server's authenticity cannot be established.

## GHSA-5cjw-f85j-cv6r · tornado

2026-11-13 · Python · **LOW** 3.1 · Pattern: `OPEN_REDIRECT→PHISHING`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an insufficient validation of redirect URLs, specifically how `_scheme_and_netloc` handled URLs with multiple leading slashes (e.g., `///example.com`). Browsers would interpret `///example.com` as an absolute URL to `example.com`, while `urllib.parse.urlsplit` in Python would incorrectly parse it as a relative URL with an empty netloc, bypassing the intended security check that prevented scheme or netloc changes.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious URL that, when clicked by a victim, would redirect them to an arbitrary external website controlled by the attacker. This could be used for phishing attacks, tricking users into revealing credentials or downloading malware.

### Key diff

```
--- a/tornado/web.py
+++ b/tornado/web.py
@@ -4251,5 +4269,10 @@ def _scheme_and_netloc(url: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
     Both are empty for a relative URL. Raises `ValueError` for some malformed
     URLs.
     """
-    parts = urllib.parse.urlsplit(_normalize_url_like_browser(url))
+    url = _normalize_url_like_browser(url)
+    # urlsplit follows RFC 3986, in which "///example.com" has an empty netloc,
+    # but browsers resolving it against an http or https URL skip any number of
+    # slashes before the host. Collapse them so urlsplit sees the same host.
+    url = re.sub(r"^//+", "//", url)
+    parts = urllib.parse.urlsplit(url)
     return parts.scheme.lower(), parts.netloc.lower()
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `_scheme_and_netloc` function to normalize URLs by collapsing multiple leading slashes (e.g., `///` to `//`) before passing them to `urllib.parse.urlsplit`. This ensures that the URL parsing aligns with browser behavior, correctly identifying the scheme and netloc for such URLs and preventing the bypass of the redirect validation logic. Additionally, it adds a check for forbidden characters in the redirect URL.

## GHSA-736f-c93q-9fwx · russh

2026-11-13 · RUST · **LOW** 2.2 · Pattern: `RACE_CONDITION→TOCTOU`

### Root cause

The `russh` SSH agent client had a race condition where an asynchronous confirmation (e.g., `read_success().await?`) could complete after the agent was locked. This meant that a signing operation, which should have been blocked by the lock, could still proceed if its confirmation arrived after the lock was applied but before the signing operation itself was fully processed and rejected.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially bypass the agent's lock mechanism, allowing unauthorized signing operations to occur even when the agent was explicitly locked. This could lead to unauthorized use of SSH keys.

### Key diff

```
--- a/russh/src/keys/agent/client.rs
+++ b/russh/src/keys/agent/client.rs
@@ -218,8 +218,7 @@ impl<S: AgentStream + Unpin> AgentClient<S> {
         let len = self.buf.len() - 4;
         BigEndian::write_u32(&mut self.buf[..], len as u32);
 
-        self.read_success().await?; 
-        Ok(())
+        self.read_success().await
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `AgentClient` methods to directly return the result of `read_success().await` or `read_response().await` instead of wrapping it in `Ok(())`. For the `extension` method, it explicitly checks for `SUCCESS` or `EXTENSION_RESPONSE` messages, treating any other response (like `FAILURE`) as an `Error::AgentFailure`. Additionally, the `kex_done` handler in the client is now called after the server key has been accepted, preventing the shared secret from being exposed for rejected servers.

## GHSA-4j63-6c4x-wfg4 · soupsieve

2026-11-13 · Python · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the inefficient handling of general sibling combinators within the ':has()' pseudo-class. When processing untrusted HTML, the existing logic for matching siblings could lead to quadratic time complexity, especially with deeply nested or complex structures, due to repeated traversals without proper caching.

### Impact

An attacker could craft malicious HTML input that, when processed by Soup Sieve, would consume excessive CPU resources, leading to a denial of service for applications using the library. This could make the application unresponsive or crash it.

### Key diff

```
--- a/soupsieve/css_match.py
+++ b/soupsieve/css_match.py
@@ -533,6 +533,7 @@ def __init__(
         self.enable_cache = not bool(self.flags & util.NOCACHE)
         self.iframe_restrict = False
         self.nth_cache: dict[Hashable, dict[Hashable, list[int]]] = {}
+        self.sib_cache: dict[Hashable, dict[Hashable, int]] = {}
 
         # Find the root element for the whole tree
         doc = scope
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a caching mechanism for general sibling combinator matching. A new 'sib_cache' dictionary is added to store the results of sibling searches, preventing redundant computations. The 'match_general_sibling' method is refactored to utilize this cache, significantly improving performance for repeated sibling lookups.

## GHSA-63hm-g96r-2m2q · laravel/framework

2026-11-13 · PHP · **LOW** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The concurrent process execution in Laravel did not properly validate or sign the output received from child processes. An attacker could inject arbitrary data into the child process's standard output, which would then be parsed as a legitimate result by the parent process, potentially leading to unexpected behavior or data manipulation.

### Impact

An attacker could manipulate the perceived outcome of a concurrent task by injecting crafted JSON into the child process's output, making the parent process believe a task succeeded or failed in a specific way, or even altering the returned result data.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Illuminate/Concurrency/ProcessDriver.php
+++ b/src/Illuminate/Concurrency/ProcessDriver.php
@@ -41,18 +48,12 @@ public function run(Closure|array $tasks): array
             }
         })->start()->wait();
 
-        return $results->collect()->mapWithKeys(function ($result, $key) {
+        return $results->collect()->mapWithKeys(function ($result, $key) use ($envelopeKeys) {
             if ($result->failed()) {
                 throw new Exception('Concurrent process failed with exit code ['.$result->exitCode().']. Message: '.$result->errorOutput());
             }
 
-            $output = $result->output();
-
-            if (($pos = strpos($output, "\x1f\x8b")) !== false) {
-                $output = substr($output, 0, $pos);
-            }
-
-            $result = json_decode($output, true);
+            $result = $this->parseEnvelope($result->output(), $envelopeKeys[$key]);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces HMAC-based signing for the output envelope of concurrent processes. A unique HMAC key is generated for each task and passed to the child process as an environment variable. The child process then signs its JSON output with this key, and the parent process verifies the signature before parsing the result, preventing output injection.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 79 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, Ruby, JavaScript, Python, Java, Erlang, PHP, C#, RUST
> → GHSA-q5jf-9vfq-h4h7

> → GHSA-8f9r-gr6r-x63q
> → GHSA-3xxc-pwj6-jgrj

## GHSA-mh32-jxcr-mh9f · astro

2026-11-13 · JavaScript · **LOW** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Astro framework allowed dynamic tag names in components to be rendered directly into HTML without proper sanitization. An attacker could inject attribute-like strings into the tag name, which would then be parsed as attributes by the browser, leading to Cross-Site Scripting (XSS).

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious client-side scripts into the rendered HTML, potentially leading to session hijacking, defacement, or redirection to malicious sites.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/astro/src/runtime/server/render/streaming.ts
+++ b/packages/astro/src/runtime/server/render/streaming.ts
@@ -171,6 +172,7 @@ export async function renderStreaming(
 
 		// HTML element (e.g. 'div', 'span').
 		if (typeof type === 'string' && type !== ClientOnlyPlaceholder) {
+			const tag = sanitizeElementName(type);
 			const props = vnode.props;
 			// Check for attributes without copying props into a new object.
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `sanitizeElementName` function that validates and sanitizes dynamic tag names. This function extracts only the valid element name part from the input string, discarding any injected attributes or invalid characters, ensuring that only a legitimate HTML tag name is rendered.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 153 occurrences**
> Languages: Python, JavaScript, PHP, Java, Go, RUST, Ruby
